10692 ms raw paused sum		 60333 ms
same TP-served rows
63740 ms all BL observed · 123 fetches		 102.95 MB
TP EFFECTIVE ASSET WAIT
incl. fall-through cost; not wall-clock		 8283 ms BL overlap-sum 60333 ms

union of overlapping request waits		 1194 ms 3522 ms
same TP-served rows

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 7542 ms + 731 ms + 10 ms = 8283 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 2409 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 10692 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 8283 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 8283 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 60333 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 1194 ms, comparable BL 3522 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 63740 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 0 ms + 446 ms + 1 ms = 447 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 1 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 447 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 447 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 447 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 61460 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 447 ms, comparable BL 3339 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 64456 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 31682 ms + 786 ms + 8 ms = 32476 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 4569 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 37046 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 32476 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 32476 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 60333 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 1664 ms, comparable BL 3522 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 63740 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 0 ms + 418 ms + 1 ms = 419 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 1 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 420 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 419 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 419 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 61460 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 420 ms, comparable BL 3339 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 64456 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 9953 ms + 703 ms + 9 ms = 10664 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 1438 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 12103 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 10664 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 10664 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 60333 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 935 ms, comparable BL 3522 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 63740 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 0 ms + 403 ms + 1 ms = 403 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 1 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 405 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 403 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 403 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 61460 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 405 ms, comparable BL 3339 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 64456 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 16870 ms + 0 ms + 8 ms = 16878 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 6102 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 22980 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 16878 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 16878 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 60333 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 1406 ms, comparable BL 3522 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 63740 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 0 ms + 0 ms + 1 ms = 1 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 7 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 8 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 1 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 1 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 61460 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 8 ms, comparable BL 3339 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 64456 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Legend
  • Baseline — page run with recorded static assets fulfilled from the local isolated asset store. Live document/API requests may still go to origin.
  • Baseline-CDP — same CDP fulfill rig as TP for manifest assets, but bytes come from the recorded local asset store instead of live origin.
  • TokenPack — browser Service Worker serves manifest assets from packed PNGs. No Playwright/CDP per-asset fulfill path is included.
  • Δ TP vs BL (prod fairness) — TP minus baseline. Real comparison including harness handicap. If TP wins here, it wins despite the rig.
  • Δ TP vs BL-CDP (tech fairness) — TP minus baseline-CDP. Both pay harness tax, so this isolates pack-vs-direct. Tech-level win.
  • SW TP measurement — direct Service Worker measurement. The report does not apply the old CDP harness-tax projection in this mode.
